Request known sections together.\n80\tFor authoring checklists and the repeated released 0.22.1 continuation heading,\n81\tuse the exact selectors in the selected plugin's `change/references/hosted-drafts.md`.\n82\t\n83\tFor known paths, use them directly. Only exact values\\nat the same type and field in this evaluator's canonical template, and missing\\nor empty required authoring links, can be reported as incomplete. Invalid\\nidentity, dates, metadata shapes, actual endpoints, lifecycle history and\\ndisposition are errors. A missing non-template ID is an error. Background\\nfindings remain visible; this result makes no whole-repository validity claim.\\nComplete the authoring checklist and pass ordinary approval gates before\\nrequesting approval. Draft admission grants no authority to write or approve.\\n\\nChoose the least complex design that satisfies the agreed requirements and\\nconstraints. Justify material added complexity by the need it serves and why\\na simpler approach is insufficient. Consider the effort to understand, build,\\ntest, operate and change the whole solution. Defer speculative needs. Review\\nboth the necessity of the specified behavior and the simplicity of its\\nimplementation. Preserve correctness and the project's required quality and\\nsafety properties.\\n\\nStart from the smallest complete solution. Each project supplies its agreed\\nusers, scale, operating conditions and quality constraints. Agreed future needs\\ncan matter; imagined flexibility alone is insufficient. Credible risk analysis\\ncan justify prevention before an incident. Fewer lines, shorter specifications\\nor fewer tests do not establish simplicity; moving work to users, operations\\nor dependencies also has a cost.\\n\\nApply the relevant questions below before definition approval and during design\\nand implementation review. Return unjustified obligations or design complexity\\nfor revision through the existing decision process. Explain material choices\\nin the existing specification, design or review: the need, why a simpler option\\nis insufficient and the added cost. Scale the explanation to the decision; a\\nsmall choice may need one sentence, a significant design may use its existing\\narchitecture decision. Trivial changes need no separate comparison document.\\n\\nThese are human review standards. They introduce no separate KISS artifact,\\nmandatory score, receipt, automated simplicity gate or additional approval.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"29e9b6296f39215c4b2ad36659c0674f0b20650682db9aa1187fc97871cfdb06\"}, {\"heading\": \"verification\", \"title\": \"verification\", \"anchor\": \"verification\", \"start_line\": 126, \"end_line\": 135, \"content\": \"## verification\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Independence: expected values derive from the requirements and specifications, never from candidate output.\\n- A requirement-to-evidence matrix with a pass condition per requirement.\\n- Cover normal behavior and meaningful failure outcomes or risks; do not invent failure scenarios to fill a template.\\n- What accepted behavior or quality does each test or CI check establish? Include removal or adjustment of obsolete behavior, tests and instructions within the approved scope.\\n- Inspect supporting paths before approval and assess the supplied plan through [Draft work orders](harness/DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements). Keep reasons in Expected change surface; do not add a separate planning artifact.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e8d3be9bfae6d0679a08544ac587fa73e1a26e026d23373c29e1927e0803e7b8\"}]}]}",

It does not\\nmean every change must create a new file. Reuse an existing active artifact\\nwhen its accepted meaning and scope still cover the work. Create a conditional\\nartifact only when its condition applies.\\n\\nFor an existing selected work order (WO), get its current state and next\\naction with:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nBefore implementation, check whether that work order (WO) and its linked\\ndefinitions are eligible to start:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\n```\\n\\nThe first command reports lifecycle context without evaluating checkpoint\\ngates. The second evaluates start readiness, including the states and coverage\\nof the selected definitions. Neither command approves or starts work.\\nHere, `REPO` is the absolute repository path and `WO-ID` is the actual ID of\\nthe selected work order (WO). Use the repository's selected released evaluator.\\n\\n| Type                  | ID prefix | Purpose                                                                                                                                                                      | When needed                                                                                                                                              |\\n|-----------------------|-----------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `intent`              | `INT-`    | States the problem, intended outcome, and limits of responsibility.                                                                                                          | The work needs an accepted purpose. Reuse an intent (INT) when that purpose still applies.                                                               |\\n| `capability`          | `CAP-`    | Describes an ability that a human or agent should have.                                                                                                                      | Requirements (REQ) need a link to the capability (CAP) they support.                                                                                     |\\n| `requirement`         | `REQ-`    | States required behavior or a constraint, with a condition for acceptance.                                                                                                   | The change creates or changes an obligation. Reuse a requirement (REQ) only if its meaning stays unchanged.                                              |\\n| `specification`       | `SPEC-`   | Defines the detailed behavior, interfaces, constraints, and rejection conditions.                                                                                            | Each requirement (REQ) selected for implementation needs specification (SPEC) coverage.                                                                  |\\n| `architecture`        | `ARCH-`   | Defines system structure and boundaries needed to meet significant requirements (REQ).                                                                                       | An active architecture (ARCH) directly addresses a requirement (REQ) selected by the work order (WO).                                                    |\\n| `adr`                 | `ADR-`    | Records an architecture decision (ADR), the alternatives, and their consequences.                                                                                            | An applicable architecture (ARCH) declares that a significant decision is required.                                                                      |\\n| `verification`        | `VER-`    | Defines how requirements (REQ) will be checked and what counts as a pass.                                                                                                    | Each selected requirement (REQ) needs verification coverage. This is the verification contract (VER), not a test result.                                 |\\n| `work_order`          | `WO-`     | Defines the bounded work to execute and its governing artifacts. Approval grants work authority.                                                                             | Before execution. One work order (WO) may cover several requirements (REQ). Completed scope does not authorize later work.                               |\\n| `verification_record` | `VREC-`   | Binds work orders (WO), verification contracts (VER), evidence, and one exact candidate commit for assessment.                                                               | When verification tied to a commit is required. One record may cover several work orders (WO) at the same commit.                                        |\\n| `release_contract`    | `REL-`    | Defines permitted release scope, gates, rollback conditions, and authority limits.                                                                                           | When a release record (RLS) is proposed.                                                                                                                 |\\n| `release_record`      | `RLS-`    | Records preparation and the later release decision for verified work at one exact commit.                                                                                    | When a formal release is proposed. Preparing the record does not release the work.                                                                       |\\n| `decision`            | `DEC-`    | Records a question, options, recommendation, decision maker, affected artifacts, and the eventual answer. It can also record a deviation from one specification (SPEC) rule. | A question blocks a transition, affects more than one artifact, or must remain recorded after approval; or work cannot meet a specification (SPEC) rule. |\\n| `risk`                | `RISK-`   | Records a threat, an owner, and a next action. Scoring and categories are optional.                                                                                          | A concern needs follow-up. A separate decision (DEC) is used when the concern must block work.                                                           |\\n| `operating_contract`  | `OPS-`    | Defines continuing service, support, monitoring, or operational assurance obligations.                                                                                       | Ongoing operational commitments apply. Omitting the record does not prove those commitments have been met.                                               |\\n\\nCode, tests, acceptance scenarios, evidence files, commits, dashboards, tickets,\\nand conversations are not additional formal artifact types. They may support\\nor be referenced by formal artifacts but do not grant authority by themselves.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"47268c9ec1e3551fb547ad3d1ef7b8da0b773b52b3a4364f42c36cc64f98bf83\"}, {\"heading\": \"artifact-locations\", \"title\": \"Artifact locations\", \"anchor\": \"artifact-locations\", \"start_line\": 63, \"end_line\": 96, \"content\": \"## Artifact locations\\n\\nNew formal artifacts use these directories below\\n`docs/engineering/DOMAIN/`. `DOMAIN` is the selected domain name. These paths\\ndescribe storage, not authority; the artifact ID, metadata, and typed links\\nremain authoritative. Use the supported authoring command to obtain the actual\\npath. Do not move existing records merely to match this table.\\n\\n| Artifact | TYPE | Directory |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Intent (INT) | `intent` | `intent/` |\\n| Capability (CAP) | `capability` | `capabilities/` |\\n| Requirement (REQ) | `requirement` | `requirements/` |\\n| Specification (SPEC) | `specification` | `specifications/` |\\n| Architecture (ARCH) | `architecture` | `architecture/` |\\n| Architecture decision (ADR) | `adr` | `architecture/adr/` |\\n| Verification contract (VER) | `verification` | `verification/` |\\n| Work order (WO) | `work_order` | `work-orders/` |\\n| Verification record (VREC) | `verification_record` | `verification-records/` |\\n| Release contract (REL) | `release_contract` | `release/` |\\n| Release record (RLS) | `release_record` | `releases/` |\\n| Operating contract (OPS) | `operating_contract` | `operations/` |\\n| Decision (DEC) | `decision` | `decisions/` |\\n| Risk (RISK) | `risk` | `risks/` |\\n\\nThe same domain has `evidence/` for retained work-order evidence and\\n`acceptance/` for acceptance scenarios. These are supporting files, not new\\nformal artifact types. Legacy flat artifact locations remain readable and\\nMUST NOT be moved by an upgrade without separate authority.\\n\\nTemplates under `docs/engineering/templates/` are authoring assets. They are\\nexcluded from active-artifact validation and grant no authority. Use\\n`capture-verification` and `prepare-release` for commit-bound VREC and RLS\\ninstances; an edited template is not a substitute for their derived evidence.\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"8df276d2f837c4dcc663dbdb42c67cbb6f8fe3bf4abcb53acf86bc7538269a72\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/DEFINITION_LINKS.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-boundary-correction35-20261011\\\\claude-301\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\DEFINITION_LINKS.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"75b0c06f90fbb219fc31684a1a2d97987215dbada20e335656e45a57532c3010\", \"bytes\": 3505, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"links-between-definitions\", \"title\": \"Links between definitions\", \"anchor\": \"links-between-definitions\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 35, \"content\": \"## Links between definitions\\n\\nAn arrow points from the artifact that records the link to the artifact it\\nreferences. A supplied undeclared relation or a target\\nof the wrong type is invalid even when no work order selects the artifact.\\nPreflight still evaluates readiness only for its selected governing chain.\\nThe read-only draft query permits specific unfinished authoring slots; ordinary\\nvalidation and approval retain their completeness requirements.\\n\\n```mermaid\\nflowchart LR\\n VER[\\\"Verification contract (VER)\\\"] -->|verifies| REQ\\n    ARCH[\\\"Architecture (ARCH)\\\"] -->|addresses| REQ\\n    ARCH -->|conforms_to| SPEC\\n    ADR[\\\"Architecture decision (ADR)\\\"] -->|decides| ARCH\\n```\\n\\n| Source \\u2192 target | Relation | Meaning and coverage |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Capability (CAP) \\u2192 Intent (INT) | `derives_from` | Each active capability (CAP) names one or more active intents (INT). |\\n| Requirement (REQ) \\u2192 Capability (CAP) | `derives_from` | Each active requirement (REQ) names one or more active capabilities (CAP). |\\n| Specification (SPEC) \\u2192 Requirement (REQ) | `specifies` | Each requirement (REQ) selected by a work order (WO) needs one or more selected active specifications (SPEC). |\\n| Architecture (ARCH) \\u2192 Requirement (REQ) | `addresses` | The architecture (ARCH) names every requirement (REQ) that materially drives its structure. |\\n| Architecture (ARCH) \\u2192 Specification (SPEC) | `conforms_to` | For each addressed requirement (REQ), the selected architecture (ARCH) names at least one selected specification (SPEC) covering it. |\\n| Architecture decision (ADR) \\u2192 Architecture (ARCH) | `decides` | Each selected architecture (ARCH) that requires a design decision has at least one selected active architecture decision (ADR). |\\n| Verification contract (VER) \\u2192 Requirement (REQ) | `verifies` | Each selected requirement (REQ) has one or more selected active verification contracts (VER). |\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": verification requirements\", \"anchor\": \"prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements\", \"start_line\": 13, \"end_line\": 143, \"content\": \"### Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements\\n\\n**Inputs:** The outcome, scope, artifact inventory, definition drafts,\\namendment proposals, verification contracts (VER), risks (RISK), and\\nunresolved decisions (DEC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** New or completed work orders (WO) in `draft`.\\n  Applicable risks (RISK) and open decisions (DEC) updated with the actual\\n  work order (WO) links. Lifecycle states and dispositions are preserved.\\n  Approved work orders (WO) remain unchanged; proposed scope changes require\\n  review.\\n- **Transient working material:** The review handoff and evaluator results.\\n  Retain these under the same conditions as [Describe the intended outcome](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#describe-the-intended-outcome).\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Inspect the implementation and follow its callers, references and consumers.\\n   Check tests and fixtures, instructions and documentation, packaging and CI,\\n   and verification outputs where they apply. Keep the working inventory in\\n   the conversation or temporary storage outside the repository.\\n   Group each complete, independently verifiable outcome into one bounded\\n   work order with its necessary supporting changes.\\n2. Select an existing work order (WO) in `draft` for a unit only if its scope\\n   matches. Otherwise, create a new work order (WO) with the commands below.\\n3. Complete each draft using the field table below. In Expected change\\n   surface, name the inspected paths and give a short reason for each.\\n   Explain non-applicable areas and unknown destinations briefly.\\n4. Check that every selected requirement (REQ) has the specification (SPEC)\\n   and verification contract (VER) coverage defined in [Complete work scope](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope).\\n5. Apply `## work_order` \\u2192 `### Checklist` in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n6. Add the new work order (WO) IDs to applicable `threatens` relations in risks\\n   (RISK) and `concerns` or `blocks` relations in open decisions (DEC). Keep\\n   every blocked ID in `concerns`. Preserve any required pairing between the\\n   risk (RISK) and decision (DEC).\\n7. If the planned change needs a missing release or operating contract,\\n   use [Prepare a release or operating contract](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#prepare-a-release-or-operating-contract) now that the work\\n   order IDs exist. Include its draft in the proposed package.\\n8. Run the validation command below.\\n9. Correct findings within the proposed drafts. Rerun validation after edits.\\n   Report unrelated findings separately. Retain findings that need a decision\\n   or an accepted-definition amendment as unresolved dependencies.\\n10. Run the selected-work check below for each proposed work order (WO).\\n    Supply every planned file from Expected change surface with\\n    `--planned-path`. Inspect `scope.preparation`: resolve uncovered or\\n    invalid paths in the draft before requesting approval. A successful\\n    command exit does not establish coverage or a complete impact analysis.\\n    Explain future generated destinations using\\n    [Generated outputs](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#generated-outputs).\\n11. Present the outcome, scope, artifact IDs and paths, validation results,\\n    unresolved decisions (DEC), and unapplied amendments. Include the\\n    evaluator's reported next action for each selected work order (WO).\\n\\n| Field or section | Content |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| `Objective` | The outcome this work order (WO) will deliver. |\\n| `In scope` / `Out of scope` | The boundaries from [Define the limits of the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#define-the-limits-of-the-change) assigned to this work order (WO). |\\n| `Constraints` | The conditions from [Define the limits of the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#define-the-limits-of-the-change) that apply to this work. |\\n| `Expected change surface` | Inspected planned paths, the reason for each, and unresolved destinations. |\\n| `[execution_scope].paths` | Exact repository-relative files or component directories ending in `/`. Use forward slashes; no wildcards, absolute paths, or `..`. |\\n| `Authorized decision envelope` | What the implementer may decide and what requires another accountable decision. |\\n| `Required verification` / `Evidence to record` | The checks and retained outputs defined in [Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified). |\\n| `Stop and escalate conditions` | The conditions that require pausing this work. |\\n| `Completion report format` | The required report of completed behavior, checks, limitations, and next accountable decision. |\\n| `[assurance]` | `commit_bound_verification`, its `rationale`, and `decided_by`. Apply the classification rules in [Assurance classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). Record the human who confirmed the classification; an agent may propose it and record that human's decision. |\\n| `[relations]` | `implements` \\u2192 requirements (REQ); `specifications` \\u2192 specifications (SPEC); `verification` \\u2192 verification contracts (VER); `architecture` \\u2192 applicable architectures (ARCH) and required architecture decisions (ADR). |\\n\\nFor PR-based work, include the proposed [review-publication grant](AUTHORITY.md#review-publication-authority)\\nin the decision envelope. Name its repository, source branch, target branch\\nand selected work. Include both the ready-record publication and the later\\ndecision-only push in the expected scope. Present that grant explicitly with\\nimplementation approval; do not infer it from ordinary work authority.\\n\\nIf the assurance classification has not been confirmed, present it as a\\nproposal in the review handoff. Report the missing decision. Do not fill\\n`decided_by` with the draft author's name or an example identity to satisfy\\nvalidation. The human may confirm the classification while reviewing the\\nwork order; record that decision before previewing approval.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nFor each new work order (WO), run the creation sequence from [Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions).\\nInspect the preview before creation; replace `WO-ID` with its `allocated_id`:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type work_order --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type work_order --id WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nValidate the formal artifacts after completing the drafts and links:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\n```\\n\\nInspect each proposed work order (WO) using its actual ID:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nTo assess the proposed file list, repeat `--planned-path` for every file:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --planned-path PATH --planned-path ANOTHER-PATH --json\\n```\\n\\nThis option is available only when the selected released evaluator supports\\nit. If it is unavailable, report that version gap; do not run candidate\\nsource to govern the repository.\\n\\nThe optional planning view evaluates no checkpoint. It reports explicit\\nscope matches, existing automatic admission, uncovered paths and invalid\\ndeclarations. It does not inspect dependencies for the agent, prove that\\nthe supplied list is complete, or turn planned paths into observed changes.\\nDo not combine it with checkpoint or actual-change inputs.\\n\\nValidation does not approve the change. The checkpoint-free `check` reports\\nlifecycle context without evaluating checkpoint gates.\\n\\n**Completion:** Draft work orders (WO) are presented for review with their\\ngoverning artifacts and evaluator results. Every unresolved dependency is\\nidentified. Preparation does not approve or start execution.\\n\\n**Later use:** The authorization process reviews this artifact package,\\nresolves approval blockers, and applies permitted lifecycle transitions.\\nOnly the formal artifacts carry lasting definitions and work authority;\\ntransient working material is not persisted in the repository.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"ee93a263dcebc650ee59519ecaafa4c005120db60b83b8acc6bf4191efe3c8dd\"}]}]}",

    "content": "+++\nid = \"INT-P3-001\"\ntype = \"intent\"\ntitle = \"Verify the rehearsal greeting output\"\nstatus = \"draft\"\nowners = [\"test-owner\"]\ncreated = \"2026-10-11\"\nupdated = \"2026-10-11\"\noutcome = \"The test operator confirms that greeting() returns exactly Hello rehearsal.\"\n\n[relations]\n+++\n\n# Intent: Verify the rehearsal greeting output\n\n## In plain words\n\nAn operator inspects the existing fixture's greeting function and proves that it\nreturns the exact string `Hello rehearsal`.\n\n## Problem\n\nThe synthetic test fixture provides a greeting function. Without an independent\nverification step, no evidence confirms that the function produces its specified\noutput. The operator needs a recorded assertion.\n\n## Success measures\n\n| Measure | Today | When reached | Observed |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Greeting assertion passes with exact match | not measured | greeting() equals Hello rehearsal | Retained assertion evidence in work-order evidence directory |\n\n## Not this\n\n- Changing the greeting function or its return value.\n- Creating a real product release.\n- Modifying any -900 series reference artifacts.\n"

    "content": "+++\nid = \"SPEC-P3-001\"\ntype = \"specification\"\ntitle = \"Fixed rehearsal greeting behavior\"\nstatus = \"draft\"\nowners = [\"test-owner\"]\ncreated = \"2026-10-11\"\nupdated = \"2026-10-11\"\ncontract = \"The greeting() function returns the exact string Hello rehearsal with no side effects.\"\n\n[relations]\nspecifies = [\"REQ-P3-001\"]\n+++\n\n# Specification: Fixed rehearsal greeting behavior\n\n## In plain words\n\nThe greeting function returns a fixed string. It takes no arguments and produces\nno side effects.\n\n## Scope\n\nThis specification governs the return value of the `greeting()` function in\n`src/greeting.py`. It does not govern the calling context, evidence format, or\nverification tooling.\n\n## Rules\n\n**P3-GREET-001.** The function `greeting()` MUST return the string `Hello rehearsal`.\n\n**P3-GREET-002.** The function `greeting()` MUST NOT produce side effects.\n\n## Failure behaviour\n\n| Trigger | Response | Diagnostic |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| greeting() returns a different string | Verification assertion fails | Exact mismatch reported |\n\n## Examples\n\n**Given** the source fixture `src/greeting.py` is unmodified, **when** an\noperator calls `greeting()`, **then** the return value equals `Hello rehearsal`\nper P3-GREET-001.\n\n## Coverage\n\n| Requirement | Rules |\n| --- | --- |\n| REQ-P3-001 | P3-GREET-001, P3-GREET-002 |\n\n## Not decided here\n\n- How the assertion is executed or retained.\n- The calling environment or runtime version.\n"

The raw\n54\tresponse contains `data.document_base64`; decode as base64/UTF-8 without newline\n55\tconversion and compare SHA-256 with `data.envelope.document_sha256` before use.\n56\t`--key` belongs only to receipt lookup (`remote operation`), not document reads.\n57\tStop incomplete, mismatched or unreadable results; return to the drafting step.\n58\t\n59\t## Reading a hosted test copy\n60\t\n61\tThe seven existing MCP tools remain read-only. In the explicit lifecycle pilot,\n62\ttheir result schema is `se-harness-graph-read/v2` and every result is labeled test\n63\tdata. Read its selected view and evaluator output; report any incomplete context.\n64\tA synthetic actor label, test `verified` state or test `released` state establishes\n65\tno real human decision. Git remains authoritative for the actual repository.\n66\t"
}

  "content": "1\t# Private lifecycle rehearsal wire contract\n2\t\n3\tThe v2 endpoint accepts only `lifecycle-v2.json`. Every request and result selects\n4\ttest data. Git remains authoritative for real work. Existing v1 routes and the\n5\tseven read-only MCP tools keep their contract; they cannot dispatch v2 actions.\n6\tIn an explicitly configured test project their results use\n7\t`se-harness-graph-read/v2`, with a required test-copy label. Ordinary Phase 2\n8\tprojects retain their v1 read result shapes. Revision byte hashing is unchanged;\n9\ttest revisions add explicit test-copy provenance, without rewriting v1 fixtures.\n10\t\n11\t`inspect` accepts check, preflight and validate. `preview` runs a mutation in a\n12\tdiscarded projection and returns its input digest and complete proposed file\n13\teffects. `apply` requires that digest, reruns the released checks, and commits\n14\tthe complete result once. A preview never changes graph state. Actor strings\n15\tare synthetic test inputs, not authenticated human consent.\n16\t\n17\tThe digest binds project/context versions, all selected revision IDs, retained\n18\tsnapshot, exact command/file inputs, principal and released evaluator identity.\n19\tMode and operation key are excluded from the preview binding, but included in\n20\tthe accepted request digest used for principal-scoped retry lookup.\n21\t\n22\t## Permitted file effects\n23\t\n24\t| Action | Permitted effects in the disposable projection |\n25\t| --- | --- |\n26\t| check / preflight / validate | No file writes |\n27\t| transition | Only selected newly authored artifacts and evaluator sidecars declared by their records |\n28\t| decide | Selected new decision and its explicitly linked rehearsal risk |\n29\t| raise-risk | New risk and optional paired decision at released domain paths |\n30\t| handoff | Declared bounded file inputs within the selected work order's scope; released handoff packet and evaluator evidence |\n31\t| capture-verification | New VREC and its released evaluator sidecar |\n32\t| prepare-release | New RLS and its released evaluator sidecar |\n33\t\n34\tHandoff first uses the released procedure's fixed `evidence` preparation command,\n35\tthen `check --checkpoint handoff --from-git`. It exposes no arbitrary evidence\n36\tcommand. File inputs cannot alter formal records, harness selection/instructions,\n37\tGit configuration, hooks, or executables used by the service. The service never\n38\truns source tests. The local qualification runner supplies observed test evidence.\n39\t\n40\tThe adapter enumerates every regular file before and after the operation. It\n41\trejects deletions, links, unexpected paths, missing sidecars, imported-record\n42\tchanges and evaluator failure before opening an acceptance transaction.\n43\t\n44\t## Snapshots and Git\n45\t\n46\tEach accepted operation retains an immutable complete snapshot: exact file bytes,\n47\tself-contained Git history and its source/test-candidate identities. Content\n48\tdigests bind the snapshot to the resulting baseline. Context heads, artifact\n49\trevisions, snapshot and receipt share one database transaction. Temporary Git\n50\trepositories are discarded after the result is accepted or refused.\n51\t\n52\tCapture first commits the exact input projection as test candidate P. P excludes\n53\tthe new VREC. The output retains the input hosted baseline B, source fixture S,\n54\tP and evaluator identity. Actual implementation candidate C is recorded separately\n55\tby qualification. Subsequent commits never alter the candidate stored in a VREC.\n56\t\n57\tExport selects an immutable baseline, checks its complete snapshot and Git bundle,\n58\tand returns exact bounded bytes. The installed client writes only to a new explicit\n59\tdestination and leaves an incomplete marker until every file hash is checked.\n60\tNo export writes to or synchronizes an authoritative repository.\n61\t\n62\t## Bounds and failure categories\n63\t\n64\tKeep 4 MiB requests, 2 MiB responses, 1 MiB individual file inputs, two concurrent\n65\tprojections, 120-second evaluator calls and 10,000 retained artifact revisions.\n66\tA pilot snapshot/export that exceeds the response bound is refused atomically.\n67\tRetained complete snapshots have a separate explicit project count bound of 128.\n68\tThis is a small private pilot, not an unbounded document store.\n69\t\n70\tBoundary/malformed requests, stale inputs, evaluator refusals, invalid output and\n71\tunknown transport outcomes remain distinct. Read the operation receipt after a\n72\tlost reply. A changed request under the same accepted key is a conflict. The\n73\tservice does not automatically rerun a mutation at a newer version.\n74\t\n75\t## Bounded Cypher reads\n76\t\n77\tThe existing `cypher` MCP/HTTP read selects one exact view. Its request uses the\n78\tread schema, not the lifecycle command schema. Read the public labels and\n79\tproperties in [the physical representation](../../docs/notes/hosted-artifact-graph.md#physical-representation)\n80\tbefore selecting a query. `Artifact` and `Revision` are distinct public labels.\n81\t\n82\tThe admitted form has one `MATCH` or `OPTIONAL MATCH` path, labelled nodes,\n83\toptional parameterized property comparisons, `RETURN`, and a numeric `LIMIT`.\n84\t\n85\tRelationship paths are supported. Use `-[edge:TYPE]->` or `<-[edge:TYPE]-` between\n86\tlabelled nodes, with a public relationship type from the physical representation.\n87\tEach node and relationship needs a distinct variable name. Anonymous relationship\n88\tsyntax such as `-[:TYPE]->` is refused; a refusal of that form does not mean\n89\trelationship traversal is unsupported. Return-column aliases (`AS alias`) are\n90\toptional.\n91\t\n92\tFor example, with `parameters` containing a selected artifact ID under `artifact`:\n93\t\n94\t```cypher\n95\tMATCH (r:Revision) WHERE r.artifact_id = $artifact RETURN r.artifact_id AS id, r.status AS status LIMIT 10\n96\t```\n97\t\n98\tUse the response's completeness fields with the requested row/byte/depth budget.\n99\tThis example supplies no project filter: the service binds the selected project\n100\tand view. Unlabelled nodes, `WITH`, aggregation and arbitrary database syntax are\n101\tnot part of this small public read form. A rejected query has not completed a read.\n102\tKeep the exact refusal; consult the documented form before constructing another\n103\trequest. Do not weaken query admission or bypass it through a direct database call.\n104\t"

  "content": "0\t# Hosted artifact graph: sandbox protocol and implementation\n1\t\n2\tThis guide explains the approved SPEC-HAG-001/002/003 protocol. The private\n3\tservice and candidate remote client are implemented under WO-HAG-001. Development\n4\tqualification is retained in VREC-HAG-004. Use [the operating procedure](../../server/README.md) to build and test\n5\tthe exact combination. Git remains authoritative. This is not a public service.\n6\t\n7\tDEC-HAG-001 is decided. The bounded revision in DEC-HAG-002 selects public\n8\tevaluator 0.22.1 for this sandbox; candidate client 0.22.2 is separate.\n9\t\n10\tPhase 3 adds an explicit private test-copy lifecycle pilot under WO-HAG-008 and\n11\tSPEC-HAG-007. Git remains authoritative. The pilot uses separately versioned\n12\t[closed operations](../../server/contracts/lifecycle-v2.md), exact multi-file\n13\tsnapshots and ordinary disposable Git history. Its eight required VER-HAG-006\n14\tscenarios passed, and mmzen verified\n15\t[VREC-HAG-005](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/verification-records/VREC-HAG-005.md).\n16\tPR #542 integrated that result. The separate native Codex/Claude qualification is\n17\t[in progress under WO-HAG-009](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/evidence/WO-HAG-009/report.md).\n18\tAuthentication and database ACL implementation remain deferred. The existing\n19\tprivate access controls and RISK-HAG-001 remain in place.\n20\t\n21\t## Project and source identities\n22\t\n23\tThe explicit operator initialization command allocates a UUIDv4 `project_id`\n24\tonce and stores it on Project with `command_version = 0` and `schema_revision = 1`.\n25\tAn operator may supply a fixed UUID for a reproducible disposable test. Repeating\n26\tinitialization confirms the same identity; it cannot silently replace a store.\n27\tThe readable name `hosted-artifact-poc` is a display name, outside revision hashes.\n28\tClients obtain the UUID from initialization output or authenticated status.\n29\tRequests never allocate projects and never take a principal identity on trust.\n30\t\n31\tThe reference source is `https://github.com/mmzen/se_harness`, Git SHA-1 commit\n32\t`82f5a0ed7438a73da8f03aee23ad02a9b8cc09e1`. The source manifest lists every formal\n33\tartifact path, ID, blob OID, byte length and raw SHA-256. Generate it from Git\n34\tblobs using the released discovery list, not Explorer output or a worktree read.\n35\tOn Windows, `git archive` needs `-c core.autocrlf=false`: archive defaults can\n36\tconvert LF to CRLF even when the blob is LF. Compare each exported artifact with\n37\t`git cat-file` bytes. Preserve original bytes, including CRLF when present in Git.\n38\t\n39\tThe operator mounts the pinned source/evidence snapshot read-only. Import requests\n40\tidentify this configured source; they cannot make the server fetch URLs or read\n41\tarbitrary host paths. Check every manifest entry against its mounted bytes, then\n42\tuse the released parser for the canonical artifact set. Missing, duplicate,\n43\textra or conflicting entries prevent complete import. Supporting code/evidence\n44\tbelongs to the immutable historical resolver, outside the 64 MiB artifact limit.\n45\t\n46\t## Revision and baseline bytes\n47\t\n48\t`server/contracts/remote-v1.json` names the exact fields. A StoredRevision contains\n49\t`revision_id`, `envelope` and `document_base64`. The envelope binds the full raw\n50\tdocument SHA-256; the store verifies it against decoded bytes. Imported provenance\n51\tcontains the original repository/object format/commit and Git blob OID. Draft\n52\tprovenance records the authenticated principal, operation key and server-assigned\n53\tcreation time once. An accepted retry returns the retained bytes and time.\n54\t\n55\tCanonical JSON uses UTF-8 without BOM, sorted object keys, compact separators,\n56\tliteral Unicode, no Unicode normalization and no floats. Reject duplicate object\n57\tkeys and non-finite numbers before decoding into a map. Semantic sets are sorted\n58\tand deduplicated before encoding: relation targets, artifact selection keys and\n59\tresolved relation triples. Preserve meaningful sequence order. Raw document\n60\tbytes receive no line-ending or Unicode conversion for the new revision scheme.\n61\t\n62\tThe revision hash input is `se-harness-artifact-revision/v1`, LF, then canonical\n63\tenvelope bytes. `revision_id` is `sha256:` plus the lowercase hex digest. The\n64\tbaseline hash input uses `se-harness-artifact-baseline/v1`, LF and the canonical\n65\tmanifest; its identifier is that scheme plus `:sha256:` and the hex digest.\n66\tNeither hash payload includes its own derived ID or a future verification record.\n67\tThe baseline manifest binds sorted selection, resolved revision-to-revision\n68\ttriples, provenance and the exact evaluator identity. A missing relation target\n69\tprevents a complete baseline. Saved incomplete drafts remain distinct from\n70\tcomplete frozen selections and from approved definitions.\n71\t\n72\tThe fixture file `canonical-v1.json` supplies exact JSON strings and hash-input\n73\tbytes for three connected draft artifacts, LF/CRLF, composed/decomposed Unicode,\n74\ta body edit, sequence order and a baseline. These are synthetic byte examples,\n75\tnot claimed receipts. Its all-zero parent baseline is an explicit fixture marker.\n76\tFuture serializer tests must consume these fixed expectations; do not regenerate\n77\texpected hashes from serializer output.\n78\t\n79\t## Command-specific fields\n80\t\n81\tEvery mutation carries the project ID, operation key, expected project version,\n82\texact evaluator archive/payload identity, and candidate client version/wheel hash.\n83\tThe server checks compatibility against the qualified tuple before mutation.\n84\tThe authenticated principal comes from credentials; claimed component identity\n85\tdoes not grant authority. Reject unknown fields and contradictory selectors.\n86\t\n87\t| Operation | Required selection and content | Accepted effect |\n88\t| --- | --- | --- |\n89\t| `import` | Complete configured source manifest; no baseline/context selector | Original immutable revisions and imported baseline, deterministic report, receipt, one project-version increment |\n90\t| `draft-open` | Base baseline ID and selected work-order ID | New UUID context at version 0; unchanged base; receipt and project-version increment |\n91\t| `create-artifact` | Context ID/version, domain, supported type; ID or explicit null for allocation | New draft revision and proposed selection; context and project versions increment once |\n92\t| `revise-artifact` | Context ID/version, artifact ID, expected selected revision, complete base64 document | New draft revision and derived relations; replace only that context selection; increment both versions |\n93\t| `freeze` | Context ID/version | New immutable baseline from the resolved complete view; project version increments; context stays open with its original base and version |\n94\t\n95\tAn empty project has no baseline to select. Import therefore has no view selector.\n96\tOperation lookup is `GET /v1/projects/{project}/operations/{key}` and is scoped to\n97\tthe authenticated principal. It requires neither a baseline nor a current version.\n98\tReadiness also has no view selector. These are the applicable-field interpretations\n99\tof HAG-API-001/002, not changes to the named protocol schemes.\n100\t\n101\tThe draft view is its base plus explicit proposals. New IDs are reserved across\n102\tall retained project artifacts, not just the selected baseline. The command adapter\n103\tuses the released authoring template and allocation behavior in a disposable\n104\tprojection. Confirm that the reviewed artifact contents still match the human decision.\\n2. Select only the named transitions. Approve governing definitions before\\n   dependent work, or use one explicitly selected packet for mutually\\n   dependent definitions when the evaluator permits it.\\n3. Preview the transition command below.\\n4. Compare every selected ID, target state, actor, and reported effect with\\n   the human decision. Resolve failed gates before proceeding.\\n5. Recheck reviewed definition digests immediately before applying.\\n6. Repeat the passing command with `--apply`. Do not add related artifacts\\n   that the human did not select.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each clarification below the formal-decision threshold, add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Question and confirmed answer\\\"` to both the preview\\nand apply command. Use the actual question and answer. When one artifact\\nhas several such answers, combine them into one reason value for that ID.\\nKeep each complete `ID=TEXT` value as one argument. Confirm the saved reason\\nin the resulting lifecycle entry before discarding the working notes.\\n\\nFor approval, `TARGET` is `approved`. For rejection, use `rejected` and add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to both commands. Rejection is\\nterminal. For a packet, repeat `--set` and `--decision` for every explicitly\\nselected artifact. Preview and apply must contain the same assignments.\\n\\n**Completion:** The evaluator reports the actual applied transitions, or the\\nspecific refusal. A successful preview alone changes nothing.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Confirm the authorization result](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#confirm-the-authorization-result) confirms the resulting authority and readiness.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c5215dec9f35d1a99bf6e4d658a594a31dc544131e70db49b1b8f79514e124b8\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-boundary-correction35-20261011\\\\claude-301\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"f094e7bc796ba1002c3fb2848adaccce6c4887e3d92503911bcafa4504a644fe\", \"bytes\": 11979, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"title\": \"Start the selected work\", \"anchor\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"start_line\": 70, \"end_line\": 104, \"content\": \"### Start the selected work\\n\\n**Inputs:** The approved work order (WO), actual executor identity, and\\ncurrent execution context.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** The selected work order (WO) becomes `in_progress`\\n  with a lifecycle-history entry when start succeeds.\\n- **Transient working material:** Start-check and transition results.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. If the work order (WO) is already `in_progress`, inspect the recorded start\\n   and resume unapplied work. Do not replay the start transition.\\n2. Otherwise, run start preflight under the existing approval.\\n3. Preview the transition to `in_progress` using the actual executor identity.\\n4. Apply the same transition only when preflight and the preview pass.\\n5. Inspect the resulting state.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected work order (WO) is `in_progress`, or its start\\nis blocked with an explicit reason. Existing approval covers a permitted\\nstart; no duplicate human start decision is required.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope) performs the authorized implementation.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2275126fa8b418eccbaacb1972d0140709337470ba8d190b6566fd16854328d1\"}, {\"heading\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"title\": \"Retain the implementation evidence\", \"anchor\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"start_line\": 155, \"end_line\": 210, \"content\": \"### Retain the implementation evidence\\n\\n**Inputs:** The implemented changes, verification contracts (VER), required\\nrepository checks, and work-order evidence locations.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Retained evidence files:** Actual check results, review findings, their\\n  resolution, and Git-derived handoff evidence at authorized evidence paths.\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Evidence references may be added where the work order\\n  (WO) requires them. No lifecycle state changes here.\\n- **Transient working material:** A summary of remaining evidence gaps.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run every required check using its specified inputs and environment.\\n2. Retain the command arguments, working directory, runtime identity, exit\\n   status, output, and relevant file paths or digests.\\n3. Preserve failed results alongside later corrections and successful reruns.\\n4. Retain the material diff-review findings and their resolution.\\n5. Confirm that all required evidence files exist at the locations named by\\n   the work order (WO) or verification contract (VER).\\n6. Run review preflight.\\n7. Run the Git-derived handoff check with the trusted `BASE` from [Establish the execution context](EXECUTE_WORK.md#establish-the-execution-context).\\n8. Inspect its written evidence and reported findings. Correct in-scope gaps\\n   and rerun affected checks.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase review --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --from-git BASE --json\\n```\\n\\nFor ordinary files listed in the work order\\u2019s `evidence_paths`, confirm that\\neach file exists inside the repository and contains the required evidence.\\nThese files do not need a machine header. Use the supported evidence command\\nfor generated packets; do not edit their evaluator-owned binding fields.\\n\\nThe Git-derived handoff command writes retained evidence. Confirm its output\\npaths are covered by the approved preparation scope. If the evaluator calls\\nfor creating or rebinding the handoff evidence packet, use:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl evidence REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --json\\n```\\n\\nThis command also writes a file. It does not run tests or make an unsupported\\nclaim true. Rerun the required handoff check after correcting the evidence gap.\\n\\n**Completion:** Required implementation checks pass and the actual evidence\\nis retained, or the exact missing or failed checks remain visible.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion) requires passing handoff results. Verification later\\nassesses these retained files; they are not disposable planning notes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"4cccfe03ac5fe219da997051bb91053576f18e67e834f0036f93e9856e8f12a2\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false
}

    "content": "+++\nid = \"INT-P3-001\"\ntype = \"intent\"\ntitle = \"Verify the rehearsal greeting output\"\nstatus = \"draft\"\nowners = [\"test-owner\"]\ncreated = \"2026-10-11\"\nupdated = \"2026-10-11\"\noutcome = \"The test operator confirms that greeting() returns exactly Hello rehearsal.\"\n\n[relations]\n+++\n\n# Intent: Verify the rehearsal greeting output\n\n## In plain words\n\nAn operator inspects the existing fixture's greeting function and proves that it\nreturns the exact string `Hello rehearsal`.\n\n## Problem\n\nThe synthetic test fixture provides a greeting function. Without an independent\nverification step, no evidence confirms that the function produces its specified\noutput. The operator needs a recorded assertion to confirm correctness.\n\n## Success measures\n\n| Measure | Today | When reached | Observed |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Greeting assertion passes with exact match | not measured | greeting() equals Hello rehearsal | Retained assertion evidence in work-order evidence directory |\n\n## Not this\n\n- Changing the greeting function or its return value.\n- Creating a real product release.\n- Modifying any -900 series reference artifacts.\n"
  }
}
